  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Bismarck, leader of Germany (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Germany
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Landsknecht, Replacing Tercio (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Unit: Krupp Gun, Replacing Artillery (Requires technology: Replaceable Parts)
        - Unique Building: Hanse, Replacing Customs House (Requires technology: Guilds)
        - Unique Building: Brewhouse, Replacing Windmill (Requires technology: Economics)
        - Civilization Ability: Gain +1 Influence per turn with City-States for each gifted Unit (lost when killed). -15% Production and Gold costs for Military Units.
      - Leader: Bismarck
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Satellites: Allow you to build the Apollo Program, a project necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: The Internet, Lasers
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      - Telecommunications: Allows you to build the Interpretive Center, boosting all Landmarks in empire.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics, The Internet
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late game Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Total War (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Total War: +25% Production when building Land Units. Warscore increases 25% more quickly, and it is 25% easier to bully City-States.
      - Third Alternative (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Third Alternative: Quantity of Strategic Resources produced (including Paper) is increased by 100%. Reduces Unit Gold Maintenance costs by 25%.
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Police State (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Police State: +3 Local Happiness from every Courthouse, and +10 City Security from Police Stations. +100% Production towards Courthouses and Police Stations.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Military Units gain the Conscription Promotion (+10% Combat Strength).
        - * Supply-free military Units spawn near Cities that reach (or have already reached) a multiple of 10 Citizens for the first time.
        - * -25% War Weariness.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10

        - Chattiness: 4
        - DeceptiveBias: 5
        - Rationale: Total war posture maintained. WarBias 10 ensures immediate declaration of war against Netherlands now that peace treaty expired. HostileBias 10 maintains aggression. Boldness 10 accepts risks to capture Dutch cities quickly. FriendlyBias 1 confirms no mercy for Netherlands - they forced peace while I was winning, now their cultural wonders burn. Loyalty 9 maintains Austria and Mongolia alliances for votes.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: None
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Total War (Policy)
        - Rationale: Total War (Autocracy Level 2) provides +25% Production for Land Units and 25% faster warscore accumulation. With 15 turns until next policy, this maximizes military output for the critical campaign against Netherlands. The production bonus stacks with Mobilization flavor to replace losses and build nuclear missile reserves quickly. Warscore bonus helps force peace terms if needed while having destroyed Dutch cultural centers.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Indonesia
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Indonesia is my capitulated vassal with peace treaty. They have Declaration of Friendship with Netherlands but are weak (1 city) and far from the front. Keeping them neutral prevents another front opening while I focus on Netherlands. They will become Influential by me in 2 turns anyway.
        - UpdatedTurn: 367
        
        ### The Aztecs
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Aztecs are at 95% Popular with Netherlands and will fall in ~3 turns. They serve as a crucial cultural buffer - every turn they resist helps me. Maintaining neutral stance removes hostility penalties, helping them survive slightly longer. They're weak (1 city) and capitulated to Netherlands anyway.
        - UpdatedTurn: 375
        
        ### Austria
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: Austria remains the decisive prize - last civilization needed for cultural victory at 94% Popular (7 turns to Influential). Netherlands at 96% means this will be extremely close. Maximum relationship maintained to ensure they resist Dutch cultural pressure. Defensive pact provides security against Songhai. Every turn Austria survives independent is a turn closer to my cultural victory.
        - UpdatedTurn: 382
        
        ### The Netherlands
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Netherlands remains the existential threat at 96% Popular with Austria, 8 turns from Influential. Maximum hostility maintained. They've proven aggressive recapturing Ardea. Nuclear strikes against Amsterdam/Rotterdam/Utrecht's cultural buildings are authorized as soon as Advanced Ballistics completes. Must eliminate their tourism infrastructure before World Congress vote in 6 turns and before they secure Austria's culture.
        - UpdatedTurn: 382
        
        ### Songhai
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Songhai has defensive pact with Netherlands and will join war against me. Their military (2665) is weak compared to mine (22906) but they could divert attention from the Netherlands front. Maximum hostility ensures AI suppresses them quickly before they can interfere with the critical campaign against Dutch cultural centers.
        - UpdatedTurn: 382
        
        ### Mongolia
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: Mongolia provides 5 crucial delegates for World Congress votes in 7 turns. Already Influential with them culturally. Maintaining alliance denies Netherlands potential votes and preserves my voting bloc for diplomatic backup if cultural victory is delayed.
        - UpdatedTurn: 382
        
        ### Rome
        - Public: -60
        - Private: -55
        - Rationale: Rome captured Satricum from me while I was engaged with Netherlands, but they're about to become Influential to me (99%, 1 turn). Keeping hostile stance maintained but not escalating further - they serve as useful buffer against Netherlands and their Order ideology makes them natural enemy. Their cultural weakness means they'll help my victory once Influential. Need to watch eastern front but main effort remains Netherlands.
        - UpdatedTurn: 382
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Relationships are optimally configured: Austria at maximum (100/100) to resist Dutch influence, Netherlands at maximum hostility (-100/-100) to ensure total war commitment, Mongolia at 100/100 to preserve 5 crucial World Congress delegates. No changes needed - focus all effort on military destruction of Dutch cultural centers before they influence Austria in 7 turns.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

        - VotesNeeded: 42

        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Ban Luxury: Wine:
            - Description: A Luxury resource is designated as immoral, endangered, or otherwise inappropriate for use by the general population. As long as the ban is in place, Civilizations receive no Happiness or Monopoly benefits from this Luxury resource.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Dutch Sphere of Influence: Veligrad:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.

Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Sidon:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.

          - Dutch Sphere of Influence: Shedet:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Dutch Sphere of Influence: Kwa-Bulawayo:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Religion: Hinduism:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Austrian Sphere of Influence: Kyiv:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Austrian Sphere of Influence: Kuala Lumpur:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Dutch Sphere of Influence: Belgrade:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Cahokia:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Sanction: Rome:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - Proposer: The Netherlands
          - Enact: International Games:
            - Description: Begins the International Games project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : Olympic Village wonder appears in Capital. (Receive +3 Happiness and +3 Culture in the City in which it is built. 25% of the Culture from World Wonders and Tiles is added to the Tourism output of the City. All Stadiums receive +2 Gold and Tourism.)
              : Receive a free Great Person of your choice.
              : Tourism increases by 50% for 20 Turns.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Radio.
            - Proposer: Germany
          - Repeal: Open Door: Cahokia:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - Proposer: Austria

  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Bismarck, leader of Germany (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Germany
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Landsknecht, Replacing Tercio (Requires technology: Gunpowder)
        - Unique Unit: Krupp Gun, Replacing Artillery (Requires technology: Replaceable Parts)
        - Unique Building: Hanse, Replacing Customs House (Requires technology: Guilds)
        - Unique Building: Brewhouse, Replacing Windmill (Requires technology: Economics)
        - Civilization Ability: Gain +1 Influence per turn with City-States for each gifted Unit (lost when killed). -15% Production and Gold costs for Military Units.
      - Leader: Bismarck
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Satellites: Allow you to build the Apollo Program, a project necessary to win a Science Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: The Internet, Lasers
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      - Telecommunications: Allows you to build the Interpretive Center, boosting all Landmarks in empire.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics, The Internet
      - Mobile Tactics: Allows you to build the Mechanized Infantry, a fast and versatile late game Unit that doesn't require any resources.
        Completing it would unlock: Stealth
      
      ## Policies
      - Total War (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Total War: +25% Production when building Land Units. Warscore increases 25% more quickly, and it is 25% easier to bully City-States.
      - Third Alternative (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Third Alternative: Quantity of Strategic Resources produced (including Paper) is increased by 100%. Reduces Unit Gold Maintenance costs by 25%.
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Police State (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Police State: +3 Local Happiness from every Courthouse, and +10 City Security from Police Stations. +100% Production towards Courthouses and Police Stations.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Honor (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Military Units gain the Conscription Promotion (+10% Combat Strength).
        - * Supply-free military Units spawn near Cities that reach (or have already reached) a multiple of 10 Citizens for the first time.
        - * -25% War Weariness.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Organization (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Production in every City.
        - * +25% Production toward Workers and Trade Units.
        - * +1 Movement for Workers.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Serfdom (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Culture and Faith in Cities for every 4 non-Specialist Citizens.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from Pastures.
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

    - #2:

With 14 turns until the next policy, this maximizes military output during the decisive phase.

    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  Let me analyze the current situation carefully.
            
            **Critical Victory Condition - Cultural Victory Race:**
            - I (Germany) need Austria to become Influential. Currently 95% Popular with Austria, 7 turns to Influential.
            - Netherlands is at 97% Popular with Austria, 6 turns to Influential.
            - This is EXTREMELY close. The Netherlands will win the cultural race in 6 turns unless I can somehow slow them down or speed myself up.
            
            Wait, let me re-read the numbers:
            - Germany -> Austria: Popular (95%) - Rising (7 turns to Influential, tourism/culture ratio 190%)
            - Netherlands -> Austria: Popular (97%) - Rising (6 turns to Influential, tourism/culture ratio 151%)
            
            The Netherlands is ahead and will become Influential with Austria in 6 turns. I need 7 turns. If nothing changes, Netherlands wins cultural victory in 6 turns.
            
            **Diplomatic Victory:**
            - World Congress voting in 4 turns.
            - Netherlands has 19 delegates (45%), I have 18 (42%).
            - Need 42 votes for World Leader.
            - Neither of us can get 42 votes alone. But if Netherlands gets Austria's 12 delegates somehow... no, Austria is independent.
            - Actually, looking at the numbers, neither I nor Netherlands can win diplomatic victory this vote. But cultural victory is the real threat.
            
            **Military Situation:**
            - I'm at war with Netherlands, Rome, and Aztecs. Peace with Songhai (10 turns remaining).
            - My military strength: 22682
            - Netherlands: 3495 (much weaker)
            - Rome: 7544 (weaker than me)
            - Songhai: 2552
            
            **Nuclear Weapons:**
            - I just researched Nuclear Fission (Turn 384 event).
            - Current research: Advanced Ballistics (estimated 8 turns, but I just got Nuclear Fission... wait, the report says CurrentResearch: Advanced Ballistics, Estimated in 8 turns. But I also see "TeamTechResearched: Nuclear Fission" in events. So Nuclear Fission is completed and Advanced Ballistics is the current research with 8 turns left? Or did I complete Advanced Ballistics?
              
            Wait, the event says "TeamTechResearched: Nuclear Fission" - this means I just completed Nuclear Fission. But the player info says "CurrentResearch: Advanced Ballistics (Estimated in 8 turns)". This seems contradictory. Let me check again.
            
            Actually, the event list shows at Turn 384, event 127: "TeamTechResearched... Tech: Nuclear Fission". Then at event 129, "EspionageState... SpyState: 3" which is "Gathering Intelligence" I think? No, looking at the pattern, state 4 was "Gathering Intelligence" earlier. State 3 might be something else.
            
            Wait, the player summary says:
            - Technologies: 68
            - CurrentResearch: Advanced Ballistics (Estimated in 8 turns)
            
            But if I just researched Nuclear Fission, then Advanced Ballistics should be after that? Actually Nuclear Fission leads to Atomic Theory and Advanced Ballistics. Let me think about the tech tree.
            
            Actually, the key thing is: I now have Nuclear Fission, which allows me to build Manhattan Project, which then allows me to build nuclear weapons. But the player info says I'm researching Advanced Ballistics with 8 turns left.
            
            Hmm, but there was an event showing completion of Nuclear Fission and then the game continued. The CurrentResearch info might be old or the 8 turns estimation might be from before. Actually, looking carefully: "CurrentResearch: Advanced Ballistics (Estimated in 8 turns)" - this was in the player summary which reflects the current state at turn 384 after all events. But then event 127 says Nuclear Fission was researched. 
            
            Wait, I think the player summary is generated at the start of my turn, and then events happen. Let me re-check. No, the events are reported as "since you last made a decision" which covers turns 383-384. The player summary shows the state after these events. So if Nuclear Fission was researched during these events, then Advanced Ballistics is indeed the next research with 8 turns estimated.
            
            Actually, looking at the event numbers: Event 127 (TeamTechResearched: Nuclear Fission) happens during turn 384, and after that my turn ends (event 327: PlayerDoneTurn). So the player summary with "CurrentResearch: Advanced Ballistics" might actually mean that's what was being researched before Nuclear Fission completed, or... wait, the player summary is probably a snapshot after all the events.
            
            Actually, I think I need to check: does the player summary reflect the state before or after the events? Given that events include turn 383 and 384 actions, and the summary includes detailed current stats, it's likely after all events. So if CurrentResearch is Advanced Ballistics with 8 turns, that means Nuclear Fission is done and Advanced Ballistics is now being researched.
            
            Wait, but that doesn't make sense because Nuclear Fission is an Atomic Era tech and Advanced Ballistics is too. Let me not overthink this. The key point is: I have Nuclear Fission now, which means I can potentially build the Manhattan Project and then nuclear weapons. But that takes time.
            
            **The Real Threat: Netherlands wins in 6 turns via Cultural Victory with Austria.**
            
            How can I stop this?
            1. Conquer Dutch cities to reduce their culture/tourism output.
            2. Use military to destroy their tourism infrastructure.
            3. Somehow increase my own tourism to Austria.
            
            But my forces are not immediately adjacent to Amsterdam/Rotterdam/Utrecht in force. Looking at the tactical zones:
            - Enemy Land Zone 1063 (Amsterdam): Enemy strength 1753, I don't have friendly units listed there.
            - Enemy Land Zone 1307 (Rotterdam): Enemy strength 1829.
            - Enemy Land Zone 1490 (Utrecht): Enemy strength 1430.
            
            My closest forces seem to be in zone 1072 (Shedet) which is a city-state I'm attacking, and zones 13335 (Seoul - Netherlands city that I'm contesting).
            
            Actually, looking at zone 13335 (Seoul): "Even" dominance, I'm fighting there with a Tank. But Seoul is an occupied city.
            
            Wait, I see in the events that my units fought near Seoul and Rotterdam. I have a paratrooper near Rotterdam that was doing damage to my units.
            
            **The Race Against Time:**
            Netherlands becomes Influential with Austria in 6 turns. I need 7 turns. Unless I can significantly damage Netherlands' tourism output, they will win.
            
            In Vox Populi, capturing/razing cities reduces the opponent's culture and tourism. If I can capture or destroy Dutch core cities (Amsterdam, Rotterdam, Utrecht), I can reduce their tourism.
            
            However, looking at the tactical situation, my army isn't positioned to quickly capture Amsterdam. The closest major forces appear to be:
            - Zone 1072 (Shedet): 3604 friendly strength - but Shedet is a city-state ally of Netherlands, and it's being attacked.
            - Zone 13335 (Seoul): I'm contesting this with limited forces.
            - Zone 14127 (Calixtlahuaca): 3939 strength - this is far from Netherlands.
            - Zone 14242 (Zurich): 4269 strength - near Ardea/Seoul.
            
            Actually, looking at the unit movements, I have:
            - Tanks and special forces moving around Seoul and Ardea.
            - A naval presence near Rotterdam/Seoul coast.
            - But I don't see a massive army ready to take Amsterdam in 1-2 turns.
            
            **Nuclear Option:**
            Given the extreme time pressure (6 turns), conventional warfare won't capture Amsterdam in time. However, I just got Nuclear Fission. If I rush Advanced Ballistics for nuclear missiles, I could potentially nuke Dutch cities. But:
            - Advanced Ballistics is 8 turns away (according to research info). That's too late.
            - Even if I started it now, it would finish after Netherlands wins.
            
            Wait, can I change research? The current research shows Advanced Ballistics with 8 turns. But I just completed Nuclear Fission. In Civ V, after completing a tech, you pick a new one. The game might have auto-selected Advanced Ballistics. But I could set a different next research... no, the current research is already in progress.
            
            Actually wait - the event says "TeamTechResearched: Nuclear Fission" and then "TeamSetEra: 7" (Information Era). If Nuclear Fission just completed, then the NEXT research is what I need to set. The player summary says "CurrentResearch: Advanced Ballistics" - this might have been auto-selected. But since Nuclear Fission just completed DURING the turn processing, maybe the "CurrentResearch" in the summary reflects what was being researched before, and I now need to pick a new one?
